{Dragonswrath_Swiftblades}Espadas veloces de la Ira del Dragón
{Dragonswrath_Swiftblades_descr}These men, often taken away when they are only children to remote Urulóke temples, live, breath and die for combat. The Urulóke or "Dragon Cult" is not a religion of peace or liturgical practice. For years these men drill, spar and worship in equal measure with the highest ranking members of the cult often being the most veteran and skilled warriors. The most dextrous train with the sword, the most stalwart with the shield and spear and the most ingenious with the crossbow. They form the Swiftblades, the Guardians and the Arcubalists respectively. Stripped of their identity at an early age, knowing nothing but the way of war, these renowned warriors are feared by both friend and foe alike.
{Dragonswrath_Swiftblades_descr_short}Fierce and loyal warriors wielding keen swords in each hand.

They train with the mighty cultists and wear a similar fitting armour but are drilled in the use of the duel-headed spear and shield. With the men of Dorwinion, Dale and even Khand beginning to creep closer to Rhûn's border the decision was made to make a unit who could hold back the thundering hooves of these nations. The mighty men of the Scion are the answer. Woe will befall any foolish mount who strays amongst their well drilled ranks.

{Suriut_Chariots}Carros Süri'ut
{Suriut_Chariots_descr}The Süri'ut are a confederation of tribes to the east of Rhûn that has not formally joined Sauron. Their people did not seek wealth, riches, land or glory and were not swayed by his promises. However, their latest leader (Sür), a man called Ja, seeks to expand his power and to make a vast empire. He has seen the strength which the Darïtaï and our leader wield and he has requested a military alliance! The Süri'ut are not great warriors but they are master craftsmen and they are fanatical about chariot races. As such, their chariots are second to none and vastly outclass the hay wagons of the Wainriders. Whilst Ja Sür cannot promise us any troops he has promised us these war chariots in exchange for riches and glory. We have trained our finest archers to use these great wagons and now we can ride across the plain as a thunder storm! Like the Lôke-Nar Rim, these chariots are also armed with the flaming arrows of the Dragon Cult. Our enemies will quake as the Empire of Rhûn expands!\n\nREQUIRES RHÛNNIC HEARTLANDS

¬######### KHAND
{Variag_Nobles}Nobles variag
{Variag_Nobles_descr}Khand is a land of tribes and chieftains, greatest of these are the Variags. Men who have featured upon the history of the west only twice but fierce men nonetheless. They are a warrior people and hold honour and bravery high. The bravest and greatest warriors are chosen to defend the sons of the Variag Chieftain and ruling noblemen, however only those of Variag blood can join the ranks of the nobles bodyguards. Appearing on the battlefield as 'Variag Nobles', they are swift and deadly. They wield powerful bows and excellent swords and wear the finest armour that the smithies of the tribes can offer. Although this is heavy armour, it is not like the armour of Dol Amroth or the Lonely Mountain as it has been adapted for men of the saddle and affords them speed as well as protection. The pride of the Variags is reflected in these men and their finely honed skills in archery and swordsmanship from atop their mounts are held in high esteem throughout the tribes.
{Variag_Nobles_descr_short}Excellent cavalry, clad in heavy armour and armed with keen blades and powerful bows.

{White_Horse_Followers}Seguidores del caballo blanco
{White_Horse_Followers_descr}As part of his consolidation of power, Khan Arkish implemented a system of bringing the noble sons of the greatest clans together in his camp. There they would live, ride, and learn of war alongside the Khan's own heir to forge bonds of loyalty amongst the historically combative clans. Thus far they have proven the wisdom of Arkish. They are, to a man, fiercely loyal to Orash and equally skilled in war. In honor of their commander's impressive steed, a gift from Arkish and rumored descendant of the legendary horses of old, they call themselves the White Horse Followers. The title is carried with pride even for those who depart the Followers to succeed their fathers, the new chiefs now inextricably tied to the royal dynasty of Arkish and Orash.
{White_Horse_Followers_descr_short}Orash's closest, most trusted war companions.

{Variag_Nobles_Lancers}Khuzáta variag
{Variag_Nobles_Lancers_descr}As the pastures of the Khan are both vast and ever changing, the traditional methods of border defense do not necessarily apply to the people of Khand. A watchtower or a stone wall is of no use when these nomadic people are likely to wander beyond the boundaries it protects not two weeks after it's construction was completed. Therefore, a wise Khan must employ a more dynamic force, in Khan Arkish's case, the Variag Khuzáta. These captains are responsible for ensuring that if a foreign, hostile force was to make it's way within striking range of any of the Khan's camps, it would be known about well in advance. Given the importance of this task, these captains have promoted to their positions soley because of merit and as such come from a variety of smaller tribes rather than the traditional 'nobility' of Khand. While not the finest warriors or diplomats, these men know the rolling hills and long pastures of Khand like no other.
{Variag_Nobles_Lancers_descr_short}Border guards of the everchanging Khandish steppes, armed with sharp spears.

{Variag_Nobles_Macemen}Amánta variag
{Variag_Nobles_Macemen_descr}The Khand of today is vastly changed from the times of Khan Arkish's forefathers.  Before, the clans raided and warred with each freely. One would claim dominion before falling swiftly to the next, a cycle only occasionally broken by outside subjugation to an ascendant Haradrim empire. Until recently.\n\nWith the return of the Dark Lord, the eye of Mordor has fallen upon Khand for the first time in millenia. The Lord of Men requires armies for his wars, and with Khan Arkish as his chosen vassal the fractured clans of Khand have been brought to heel one by one. But generations of infighting are not easily forgotten, and out of the furthest east there are strange rumors also; of fanatical dissidents, and mountainfolk not seen in generations.\n\nKhan Arkish is no fool, and he has learned much from his master of the art of domination. Inspired by the inquisitors of Durthang, Arkish has organized a new arm of the military under his oldest friend Yiltig. These heavily armored macemen, the Amanta, are well equipped and trained to maintain order in the burgeoning urban environments of the new Khand. Despite their intended purpose they are no less deadly on the battlefield, making them an invaluable tool against all enemies of the Khan within or without.
{Variag_Nobles_Macemen_descr_short}Brutal enforcers of the Khan wielding hardy maces and shields

{Variag_Veterans}Veteranos variag
{Variag_Veterans_descr}These veterans of the Variag have seen their share of war and battle and come away from this life with a profound love for both. They have fathered many sons of the clan and carried away more plunder than any can boast for. These are the men others look up to for guidance, for leadership, and for the best protection of enough can be found. Although their skill on horseback is unequaled; they have chosen to fight on foot, using the signature two-handed curved sword of the Variags. Their scale armor provides excellent protection from enemy arrows while their massive weapons cleave holes in their foe's battle line. These men can even stand against mounted warriors, staring down the horse of the enemy before side stepping a charge and delivering a blow powerful enough to kill rider and mount in one terrible strike.\n\nThe Variag Veterans are an unmatched force! Woe to any who counts them as an enemy and a blessing to he who claims them as an ally.
{Variag_Veterans_descr_short}Well-trained and experienced veterans, preferring to fight on foot rather than on horeseback.

{Steppe_Tribesmen}Hombres de las tribus de las estepas
{Steppe_Tribesmen_descr}Although the Variags are the strongest of all the tribes of Khand, their numbers are still mainly made up of unskilled tribesmen, little more than peasants. Due to the harsh lifestyle of the steppes, these men are much stronger than the peasants of the men of the west but they are not a problem for even militia to deal with. Variags have a rich tradition of warfare and due to their mingling with Haradrim and Easterlings they have a strong hatred toward Gondor and their northern Rohirric allies. These men use that hatred and as such can survive in battle on their dark zealotry alone. When war is called, these men choose to march forward with a spear and a shield for protection, additionally, they carry a single volley of javelins. They wear little to no armour and should avoid frontline combat, being used as a last resort or as arrow fodder.
{Steppe_Tribesmen_descr_short}Spear wielding peasantry equipped with very light armour.

{Marauders}Merodeadores
{Marauders_descr}Khand is a large country, stretching from the Ered Lithui down to the deserts of Near Harad and it is predominantly ruled over by the Variags. However there are bands of roaming mercenaries and bandits who terrorize the innocent clans folk of the steppes. Using cunning and a great deal of money, the Variags have managed to persuade these Marauders to focus their fury on the foes of the clans and they have since become powerful allies. They aren't well armoured and they aren't well organized but they are very fast, relatively cheap and perfect for picking off any fool who tries to run from the battlefield. Like vultures they prowl the field out of range of any real threat but ready to strike when a weakness opens up.
{Marauders_descr_short}Axe wielding horsemen, lightly armoured and swift.

{Nomad_Horsemen}Jinetes nómadas
{Nomad_Horsemen_descr}These horsemen, drawn from all the tribes of Khand, form the bulk of the feared mounted hordes of Khand. Like all of the men of Khand they are proud and have been shaped by a warrior culture and have known the saddle since birth becoming fine horsemen. These men only wear medium armour and, instead of the heavy lances favoured by Khand's more elite cavalry, they use spears on the battlefield. One thing these men do have in abundance is speed, and they have mastered the hit and run tactic and will not easily tire. However, due to their relatively light equipment, these men will not survive in prolonged combat long and must use their speed and superior horsemanship to quickly disengage after delivering their stinging charges.
{Nomad_Horsemen_descr_short}Medium horsemen armed with deadly spears and cavalry shields.

{Khandish_Raiders}Incursores khandinos
{Khandish_Raiders_descr}Khand, lying south west of Rhûn, has been the traditional ally of the Easterlings since the time of the Wainriders. As such they supply mercenaries to the Warlords and the Lôke-Khan whenever asked. These take the form of Horsemen and adept archers. The Khandish Hunters are fast moving archers who form a perfect counter-part to the guard of the Easterlings. Outfitted with light leather lamellar and steel helmets Hunters carry composite bows and axes into battle. In times of need, the Hunters can be mounted and serve as cavalry where their skills rival those of the Lôke-Khans own elite cavalry. When mounted, these men act like a brutal hit and run unit and as such, their enemies have branded them 'Raiders' due to the frequency of the raids conducted by these grim men.
{Khandish_Raiders_descr_short}These hardy bowmen have a fierce and unruly reputation.

{Uushixia_Stormriders}Cabalgatormentas uushixià
{Uushixia_Stormriders_descr}When the Khan chose to side with the Blue Wizards, many fearsome warriors came with the two Maia and none are more mysterious or feared than the women of the Uushixià clan. Defying all known warring convention, the clan sends its women to war alongside the men! These feared warriors are like a storm: their charge decimates enemy battalions when used correctly and their crossbows, gifted to them by the Orocarni, ensure that long after any archer has run out of arrows they will continue to fire deadly bolts that, though slow to fire, can punch through even the heaviest armour. This barrage of bolts is made even more deadly due to their mobility; only enemy cavalry stands a chance of catching up to these riders. They are a deadly foe that the Shadow would do well to fear, for they are well equipped to trample both its weaker hordes and pierce the armour of its elite troops.
{Uushixia_Stormriders_descr_short}Uushixià tribeswomen armed with powerful Dwarven crossbows and riding chariots pulled by swift steppe horses.

{Variag_Horse_Archers}Arqueros a caballo variag
{Variag_Horse_Archers_descr}The bow is a central part of life in Khand, when they are strong enough to pull the string, the youth of Khand begin practice. However, the vastness of the steppes of Khand meant their archers adapted to firing from the saddle. When the chieftains of Khand call, hordes of horse archers respond. Their mounts are bred for endurance and stamina and they will keeping running long after most men and horses can go no further. As such these men use 'hit and run' tactics almost invariably. Raining wave after wave of arrows, with deadly accuracy, upon their enemies. The horse archers of Khand are second only to the riders of Imladris but a lifetime of practice and perfection leaves these men vulnerable if engaged in prolonged melee. They are outfitted for manoeuvrability and speed and so use light armour and only short swords if the need arises.
{Variag_Horse_Archers_descr_short}Skilled horsemen, adept at firing from the saddle and outfitted for speed.

{Warlords_Kataphracts}Catafractos del señor de la guerra
{Warlords_Kataphracts_descr}Khand is a land of horsemen and many men are born in the saddle. Unlike the Rohirrim, this is more out of necessity than desire but that does not mean the men of Khand are not adept on horseback and these men are testament to that fact. After having spent a lifetime on horse and bearing the scars of countless battles, the best Khand has to offer are selected to join the Brotherhood of the Lance, one of three elite groups within Khandish life collectively known as the 'Warlord's Brotherhood'. Choosing lances over the more common Khandish spear, they are the juggernauts of the east, appearing in battle on fine mounts and wearing well crafted heavy lamellar armour, they crush all those who stand in their path. They are not as mobile as the men of Rohan and their armour slows them down but often only one charge by these formidable units is enough. They take on only the best men of the tribes and are only called into battle if absolutely necessary as their numbers are few. "Just as night falls on the Steppes, so to do we fall on our enemies; as the serpent devours all, so to do we devour our enemies; as the shadows spare none, so to do we spare no enemy. As a silver tide we rise, in the name of Khand!" - Song of the Brotherhood
{Warlords_Kataphracts_descr_short}Elite horsemen wearing the heavy armour of Khand's elites and wielding deadly lances.

They are not as skilled as the secretive Warlord's Brotherhood or as well equipped as the Variags' own hand picked troops but they are fierce fighters nonetheless. Despite their war fervour, loyalty and fierce bloodlust, they are still relatively lightly armoured and their ranks can be decimated by archer fire and should not be expected to hold against the heavily armoured infantry of Gondor or the Knights of Dol Amroth. These particular warriors are those who have chosen to fight on foot with round shields and heavy sabres and will usually form the bulk of Khand's infantry.

{Variag_Swordsmen}Espadachines variag
{Variag_Swordsmen_descr}Khand has no formal military and most men go to war with whatever weapon they have practised with the most. There are exceptions to this rule however and the warriors of Khand are testament to that. When a man has served his time in the ranks of the Khandish Elites but has not lost the taste for battle, they almost always join with the Warriors of Khand. These men have seen countless battles and retain their heavy armour from their past. As the duel swords of the elites are symbolic, the warriors can no longer use them and so, to ensure they still instil fear, they have adopted heavy two handed swords. Performing as the very best dismounted shock troopers that Khand can field, they are proud of their pasts and have little fear of death, making them a truly formidable force on the battlefield. Few men survive long enough to see their service ended in Khand, making these units incredibly rare.

{Warlords_Guard}Guardia del señor de la guerra
{Warlords_Guard_descr}Often, the field of battle is not suited to cavalry and the vast hordes of Khand have to use infantry instead, equipping their warriors with sword, spear and axe. Should a man prove his skill on the field of battle on foot then he may be called into the fighting battalions of the Brotherhood of the Axe, one of three elite groups within Khandish life collectively know as the 'Warlord's Brotherhood'. The Brotherhood of the Axe is the youngest of the four and they wear their heavy armour with pride. Opting to use an axe in honour of the great Khandish hero Hasar; Hasar fought and died in battle against Gondor in T.A 1944 but was said to have fought with the strength of 10 men; a maelstrom in the sea of men, wielding his two-handed axe with fury. In battle they also wield these mighty axes and can carve through the enemy ranks with ease, also, like all Brotherhood warriors they cover their faces so as to inspire fear. Despite their prowess, they are only called into battle if absolutely necessary as their numbers are few. "Just as night falls on the Steppes, so to do we fall on our enemies; as the serpent devours all, so to do we devour our enemies; as the shadows spare none, so to do we spare no enemy. As a silver tide we rise, in the name of Khand!" - Song of the Brotherhood

{Windriders_of_Khand}Cabalgavientos de Khand
{Windriders_of_Khand_descr}Windriders take their name very deservedly, being the fastest unit on the face of Middle-earth. It is said amongst the tribes of Khand that when the Nazgûl came to the far east to rouse the tribes for war in Sauron's name, the Windriders outpaced them. A feat only accomplished by Glorfindel of Gondolin! The Windriders are a group of warriors who now fight for the Variag's but are descended from the first tribesmen to master horse riding. As such they have spent thousands of years closely guarding their masterful secrets and practising the art of riding. Whilst not the finest warriors of the clans they are still very skilled at fighting atop their horses and they carry fierce sabres, capable of slicing through enemies at top speed. There are none faster than the Windriders and they are a formidable foe.
{Windriders_of_Khand_descr_short}Lightning fast sword wielding mounted warriors.

{Variag_Lancers}Lanceros variag
{Variag_Lancers_descr}Hailing from the south-eastern steppes of Khand, these riders follow an ancestral art of battling on horseback. These men have been trained for years with the heaviest horses found in these harsh regions, and donning with the best equipment the Khandish smithies could craft: lamellar plate armour, with a closed helmet, and a long sharp wooden lance and a small iron buckler. Their charge is among the most terrific and powerful of the warriors of the east and few enemies can withstand such tremendous power. Even the spearmen from Dol Amroth and Gondor know their fate might come if a battalion of these heavy cavalry decide to trample them.
{Variag_Lancers_descr_short}Armoured heavy cavalry equipped to break enemy formations with mighty charges.

¬######### ENTS
{Ents}Ents
{Ents_descr}These shepherds of the forest are the answer to Yavanna's prayers on behalf of the tress of Middle-earth. Created during Arda's Spring in the First Age the Ents were intended to safe-guard the forests of the world from greedy axes and ensure the survival of Yavanna's fruits. Unfortunately Melkor's and Sauron's wars wrought great destruction upon Middle-earth's forests and by the time of the Third Age the Ents became isolated and contained to Fangorn Forest. Feeling betrayed and forgotten they care not for the wars and struggles of Middle-earth's peoples, spending their time tending to the last remnants of the ancient primeval forests. As the conflict between the Free Peoples and Sauron escalates it may soon spill out into Fangorn, the Ents' last refuge, and should that come to pass the forest shepherds' wrath may become known once more. Ents differ greatly in size, appearance and form just like the different species of trees that they guard and which they tend to resemble. The one trait all Ents share is their immense strength which is said to surpass even the mighty trolls who are said to be Morgoth's imitation of Ents. Therefore, when enraged, the apparently docile forest shepherds can turn into frightening opponents able to crush most flesh and blood creatures with a single blow or swipe of their powerful limbs. However their apathy towards anything that doesn't concern their woodland domain may make stirring them into a war-like disposition difficult.
{Ents_descr_short}Antiguos centinelas del bosque con inmensa fuerza.

But Tharbad has suffered a mortal injury, and soon it will fall. It will be one more ruin of the old kingdom, silent and abandoned.\n\nThe only life that can be found there is a sad and opportunistic one. Hiding in it's sunken alleys and destroyed homes, the thieves of Tharbad wait. Though light and manoeuvrable she is still a ship of war, however, and should not be underestimated. For her size and draught she can outmatch most other vessels.

{Arainior_Caleryn}Arainior Caleryn
{Arainior_Caleryn_descr}In the wars of the Last Alliance, the headstrong King Oropher fell. Attacking rashfully, and against the advice of his retainers, much of his army would perish in the soon to be called Marshes of the Dead. These Elves were his councillors, some servants of his house since his infancy; all survivors of the heartbreak of Beleriand. Mournfully they laid their Lord to rest and swore to never allow Thranduil to suffer his father’s fate. It is a bitter oath however, as each of them knows that the same seed of pride that felled Oropher lurks within his progeny.\n\nRefugees many times over, these Elves have seen home after home abandoned to the servants of the enemy. That he might lift the shadow of failure from them and tie them to their latest dwelling, Thranduil has proclaimed these warriors “Arainior Caleryn”, Noble Elders of Greenwood. Yet in truth these Sindar do not belong in the forests of middle earth, only their oaths and their shame keep them from taking ship to the undying lands. In their hearts they hope soon they will see their last war, for good or Ill. Grim faces veiled and with weary honour they carry halberds in their Lords defence, praying they will be granted death before they see another of their charges fall.
{Arainior_Caleryn_descr_short}Noble Elders of the Greenwood

{Ahtarva_Taminnarda}Ahtarva Taminnarda
{Ahtarva_Taminnarda_descr}For their boasting belied a deep melancholy. They ached bitterly at the killing of the last of the line of Fëanor, the great jewel-smith Celebrimbor, creator of the Rings and catspaw of the Great Deceiver. In time it has come to pass that Eregion has been reclaimed by the efforts of the Eldar and though this comes as some comfort to many of the Noldor, never again shall mere jewellery be crafted in its forges. Every smith works tirelessly that the newly mobilised regiments of the Elves might be equipped, or else-wise serves in the battle line himself. Yet even amongst these new war-smiths a few stand apart. Eldar marked too deeply by the burning of Eregion and the killing of their Lord, for whom even the rebuilt splendour of its cities does little to dim their hatred. In blasphemous and dangerous imitation of their lieges of old these Noldor have done something unthinkable and as a company sworn an oath of vengeance upon the Dark Lord Sauron, to ensure the Rings are reclaimed from the hands of evil. Eschewing the blue and brilliance of their brother-smiths, in red and black the Vengeance of the Forge Realm takes the field.\n\nTheir Greathammers, made for nothing but battle, will hew down the very walls of Barad-dûr. Their fellow elves, recalling the consequences the last time such a fellowship bound themselves so, view them with suspicion if not outright hostility. Yet under the patronage of a new leader of the people of the Jewelsmiths this fell brotherhood is ever on campaign, coming rarely back to the land of the Holly, and this shields them from censure. On the battlefield their remembrance of the great losses of their people drives their compatriots to ever greater acts of valour and for a time even the most tranquil of the Noldor might feel that he is one of the sons of the Lord of the lights, at war in the shadow of Angband. Yet at battle’s end the rage passes, and the Elves are once more known to themselves.\n\nIn silent shame they watch the disciples of Dornornoston stalk from the field, having for just a moment shared in a measure of that company’s fathomless rage, and knowing what would befall any creature that stood in the path of their Oathpact.
{Ahtarva_Taminnarda_descr_short}Legendary Smiths of Eregion

{Temple_War_Lords_descr}The Black Númenóreans represent Sauron’s most potent vision for humanity. Heartless and devoid of any mercy, these men are the Dark Lords greatest enforcers of his will. Utterly devoted to their master and twisted by his dark magics, what was once a noble man of the west has long since passed into ruinous evil.\n\nMorgoth’s Temple is now Sauron’s training ground for his greatest warriors. These men have devoted themselves to perfecting the art of war. Their brutality and cunning in battle is matched only by their contempt for Sauron’s enemies. As such; they are feared by the Orcs and Uruks of Mordor, and rightly so! For if these men see their own kind as faithless dogs, imagine what brutality they unleash on those not of their kin? Orcs bow and cower before their rightful masters, lest they suffer agonizing death.\n\nWar-Lords are true masters of their craft. To ride beside the Nazgûl is the greatest honor bestowed upon them, although their sense of ‘honor’ has long since faded into bloodlust.

These were not simple soldiers, but men of repute and station. Trained warriors, highly skilled in the way of horse and war. Though Rohirrim born, their hearts and swords belonged to he who would be Twofold King, and his noble line, not the pretenders who rule from the Golden Hall now. With them came the mighty war-horses of the plains of the east, and armour leagues beyond what even a Brehin might dream of. Now, though Wulf has long since been felled by Forgoil scum, and the clans no longer are united, the Frekkalingir still keep watch over their herds in the Plains of Dunland, an ill-named region, for it is hilly still. In their great hall in Byrig, they maintain their ancestral swords and oaths, serving only the throne of the High King, no matter who is seated upon it.\n\nThey know, and have long since accepted, that none of their kin will ever enjoy the crown upon their brow, for the Rohirric taint in their blood denies them this, yet they relish in the knowledge that their power, their status, belongs to them solely because of that very same blood, the blood of those most loyal to Freca's Line! And so they wade into battle, clad in heavy leather and iron unlike any other warrior in the Hills, upon horses of war, rumoured offspring of the fabled Mearas. When they charge, they do so with the ground trembling underfoot, with Wulf's name on their lips, and with seething hatred in their hearts

{Gildor_BG}Gildorin Raphadrim
{Gildor_BG_descr}With the decline of Arnor and the awakening of Sauron in the latter Third Age, travel through Eriador has become a dangerous affair even for elves. Despite the perils, many seek to make such journeys. There are those making the final voyage to the Grey Havens and thence into the west, and others simply wish to visit kin in the Havens or gaze upon Aman through the Seeing-Stone of Elostirion. Gildor is one who has made this journey many times and in these travels he has gathered a fellowship of like-minded elves to share the road, both for company and protection. In time these companions developed into an impromptu fighting force, dedicated to guarding other elves as they make the journey westwards. To that end these Raphadrim equip themselves lightly, and wield both bow and sword to deal with whatever threats lurk along the path. While not professional soldiers, they are well-versed in the wilds of Eriador and have honed their skills over the long centuries skirmishing with the creatures of shadow. Many a lone elf caught in an ambush by orcs has been relieved to see Gildor and his Raphadrim spring from the trees upon their attackers, a cry of battle on their lips and the light of the west in their eyes.

{Lossoth_Icewalkers}Caminantes del hielo lossoth
{Lossoth_Icewalkers_descr}Life in the far north is unrelenting, and only those who call it home understand its merciless grip. What a Dalian might shiver at, or even an Angmarim might curse, the Lossoth dismiss with disdain. They wander the vast, featureless expanse of the Forodwaith, where snow stretches endlessly toward a dim horizon beneath oppressive, cloud-laden skies. What prey they stalk in that frozen expanse, none from the south can say, but their homes, fashioned from the bones of creatures large enough to dwarf a noble’s hall, whisper of the monstrous quarry hidden in the white nothingness.\n\nThe Lossoth are the true rulers of the north, after a fashion, and their blood is as cold as the lands that sustain them. They lived here long before the Númenóreans carved their fleeting kingdoms into the stone and woods, and when the last ruler of Arthedain drowned in the Bay of Forochel, their fragile realm collapsing into ruin, the Lossoth paid it no heed. They continued their hunt across the tundra, undisturbed.\n\nTo endure in such desolation is to embody it. These men are not merely hunters—they are killers forged by hardship and brutal necessity. Their hooked arrows are tipped with poisons that bring down man and beast alike, from lumbering walruses to the stranger creatures of the north. Their layered armor, stitched from fur and sinew, seems strange and cumbersome to the untrained eye, but while others freeze to death beneath the bitter winds, the Lossoth endure.\n\nTo wage war in snow and ice is to fight on their terms, and any commander with wisdom will seek their spears and arrows. But those who deal with the Lossoth would do well to remember—they are not men of hearth and warmth. They are born of the cold, their hearts as stark and unyielding as the frozen plains they call home.
{Lossoth_Icewalkers_descr_short}Foreign hunters from the cold wastes.

{Lossoth_Bodyguard}Sapputiyok lossoth
{Lossoth_Bodyguard_descr}Life in the far north is unrelenting, and only those who call it home understand its merciless grip. What a Dalian might shiver at, or even an Angmarim might curse, the Lossoth dismiss with disdain. They wander the vast, featureless expanse of the Forodwaith, where snow stretches endlessly toward a dim horizon beneath oppressive, cloud-laden skies. What prey they stalk in that frozen expanse, none from the south can say, but their homes, fashioned from the bones of creatures large enough to dwarf a noble’s hall, whisper of the monstrous quarry hidden in the white nothingness.\n\nThe Lossoth are the true rulers of the north, after a fashion, and their blood is as cold as the lands that sustain them. They lived here long before the Númenóreans carved their fleeting kingdoms into the stone and woods, and when the last ruler of Arthedain drowned in the Bay of Forochel, their fragile realm collapsing into ruin, the Lossoth paid it no heed. They continued their hunt across the tundra, undisturbed.\n\nTo endure in such desolation is to embody it. These men are not merely hunters—they are killers forged by hardship and brutal necessity. Their hooked arrows are tipped with poisons that bring down man and beast alike, from lumbering walruses to the stranger creatures of the north. Their layered armor, stitched from fur and sinew, seems strange and cumbersome to the untrained eye, but while others freeze to death beneath the bitter winds, the Lossoth endure.\n\nTo wage war in snow and ice is to fight on their terms, and any commander with wisdom will seek their spears and arrows. But those who deal with the Lossoth would do well to remember—they are not men of hearth and warmth. They are born of the cold, their hearts as stark and unyielding as the frozen plains they call home.\n\nThese men are the most experienced and battle-ready of the Icewalkers. They have endured many a freezing night by the side of their chief and protected their tribe from beast and man alike. While the population of the Lossoth people is scattered, hard times call for desperate measures and often times taking the food of your fellow tribe is easier than finding prey in the vast wilderness of Forodwaith.
{Lossoth_Bodyguard_descr_short}The Lossoth chief's most battle-ready hunters.
